# 🟢 English Speaking Course — Lesson 55

# 💬 Asking & Answering Questions Naturally

**Level:** Intermediate → Upper-Intermediate
**Main Focus:** শুধু প্রশ্নের উত্তর দেওয়া নয়—**কীভাবে naturalভাবে answer শুরু করবে, answer বড় করবে, follow-up question করবে এবং conversation চালিয়ে যাবে।**

### 🔗 Previous lesson connection

**Lesson 53 — Objects & Products**
→ জিনিস describe করা

**Lesson 54 — Daily Problems & Real-Life Situations**
→ বাস্তব situation handle করা

**Lesson 55 — Asking & Answering Questions Naturally**
→ এখন সেই situation-এ **conversation কীভাবে চালিয়ে যাবে** সেটা শিখব।

---

# 🎯 Lesson 55-এর Main Goal

আজকের lesson শেষে তুমি পারবে:

* Yes/No answer-এর বদলে বিস্তারিত answer দিতে
* answer শুরু করার natural expressions ব্যবহার করতে
* **Why / How / When / What / Where** দিয়ে answer expand করতে
* follow-up question করতে
* conversation আটকে গেলে চালিয়ে যেতে
* একই প্রশ্নের short, medium ও long answer দিতে
* নিজের কথা বলার সময় thinking expressions ব্যবহার করতে
* অন্য ব্যক্তির কথার ওপর ভিত্তি করে নতুন question করতে

---

# 🧠 Part 1 — Biggest Problem in Speaking

অনেক learner প্রশ্নের উত্তর এভাবে দেয়:

### Question:

> Do you like music?

### Answer:

> Yes, I do.

Grammar ঠিক।
কিন্তু conversation এখানেই শেষ।

### Better:

> Yes, I do. I listen to music almost every day, especially when I'm working or traveling.

আরও natural:

> Yes, definitely. I listen to music almost every day. I usually prefer relaxing music because it helps me concentrate.

এখন conversation চালানো সহজ।

---

# 🔥 Part 2 — The 3-Step Answer Formula

যেকোনো সাধারণ প্রশ্নের জন্য:

# **ANSWER → REASON → DETAIL**

### Example:

**Question:**

> Do you like your city?

**Answer:**

> Yes, I do. **(ANSWER)**
> I like it because there are many opportunities for education and work. **(REASON)**
> I also enjoy the restaurants, shopping centers, and different activities available here. **(DETAIL)**

---

# 🧠 Part 3 — Short Answer → Better Answer

### Question:

> Do you like reading?

### Level 1:

> Yes.

### Level 2:

> Yes, I like reading.

### Level 3:

> Yes, I really enjoy reading because I can learn new things from books.

### Level 4:

> Yes, I really enjoy reading, especially books about technology and personal development. I usually read in the evening because that's when I have more free time.

**Goal:** Level 3–4.

---

# 🔥 Part 4 — 5 Golden Expansion Questions

নিজের answer বড় করতে মনে মনে এই 5টা question করবে:

> **WHAT?**

> **WHY?**

> **WHEN?**

> **HOW OFTEN?**

> **EXAMPLE?**

---

### Example:

> Do you like sports?

**What?**

> Yes, I like football.

**Why?**

> I enjoy it because it's exciting.

**When?**

> I usually watch matches at night.

**How often?**

> I watch them a few times a week.

**Example?**

> I especially enjoy watching important international matches.

---

# 🧠 Part 5 — Answer Starters

সরাসরি answer না দিয়ে naturalভাবে শুরু করতে পারো:

> Well,...

> Actually,...

> Honestly,...

> Basically,...

> Personally,...

> In my opinion,...

> I would say...

> I'd say...

> As far as I'm concerned,...

---

### Example:

> Well, I'd say I prefer working in the morning.

> Actually, I don't use social media very much.

> Personally, I prefer studying alone.

---

# 🔥 Part 6 — “I'd Say”

Very useful natural expression:

# **I'd say = আমি বলব / আমার মতে**

### Examples:

> I'd say my biggest strength is consistency.

> I'd say English is one of the most important skills for my career.

> I'd say I prefer cities to villages.

---

# 🧠 Part 7 — “To Be Honest”

Opinion দিতে:

> To be honest,...

### Example:

> To be honest, I don't really enjoy crowded places.

> To be honest, I prefer studying at home.

এটি natural conversation-এ খুব common।

---

# 🔥 Part 8 — “Actually”

আগের ধারণা একটু clarify/correct করতে:

> Actually, I don't watch TV very often.

> Actually, I started learning English recently.

> I thought it would be difficult, but actually, it was easier than I expected.

---

# 🧠 Part 9 — “Well” কখন ব্যবহার করবে?

Question শুনে answer organize করতে:

> Well, that's an interesting question.

> Well, I think there are several reasons.

> Well, it depends on the situation.

এটি **thinking time** দেয়।

---

# 🔥 Part 10 — Thinking Expressions

উত্তর দেওয়ার আগে একটু সময় দরকার হলে:

> Let me think.

> That's an interesting question.

> I've never really thought about that.

> That's a difficult question.

> Let me see...

> I suppose...

---

### Example:

**Question:**

> What is the most important skill for success?

**Answer:**

> That's an interesting question. I'd say communication is one of the most important skills because...

---

# 🧠 Part 11 — “It Depends”

সব question-এর answer Yes/No নয়।

### Example:

> Do you prefer working alone or with others?

Natural answer:

> It depends on the situation. If I'm doing programming work that requires concentration, I prefer working alone. But for a group project, I prefer working with others.

🔥 This is a strong speaking technique.

---

# 🔥 Part 12 — “It Depends On...”

> It depends on the situation.

> It depends on the person.

> It depends on the time.

> It depends on the type of work.

### Example:

> It depends on the type of movie. I enjoy action movies, but I prefer documentaries when I want to learn something.

---

# 🧠 Part 13 — Follow-Up Questions

Conversation চালানোর জন্য question back করো।

### Example:

**A:**

> I really enjoy listening to music.

**B:**

> That's nice. What kind of music do you usually listen to?

**A:**

> I mostly listen to pop music.

**B:**

> Interesting. Do you have a favorite singer?

এটাই natural conversation।

---

# 🔥 Part 14 — 10 Useful Follow-Up Questions

অন্যের answer-এর পরে:

> Really? Why?

> What about you?

> How about you?

> What kind?

> Why do you like it?

> How often do you do it?

> When did you start?

> Who do you usually do it with?

> What do you like most about it?

> Would you like to do it more often?

---

# 🧠 Part 15 — “What About You?”

সবচেয়ে useful conversation phrase।

### Example:

**A:**

> I usually spend my weekends at home.

**B:**

> That's nice. What about you?

Actually, তুমি অন্য person-কে question ফিরিয়ে দিচ্ছ:

> What about you?

= **তোমার কী অবস্থা / তুমি কী করো?**

---

# 🔥 Part 16 — “How About You?”

প্রায় একই অর্থ:

> I prefer tea. How about you?

> I usually study at night. How about you?

---

# 🧠 Part 17 — Avoid One-Word Answers

### Question:

> What do you do in your free time?

❌

> Reading.

Better:

> I usually read in my free time.

Better:

> I usually read and watch videos in my free time.

Best:

> I usually read, watch educational videos, and work on personal projects when I have free time.

---

# 🔥 Part 18 — Yes/No Questions Expand করা

### Question:

> Do you like traveling?

❌

> Yes.

### Better:

> Yes, I do. I enjoy traveling because I can experience new places and meet different people.

### More natural:

> Yes, definitely. I enjoy traveling because it gives me a break from my daily routine. I also like exploring new places and trying local food.

---

# 🧠 Part 19 — WH Questions Expand করা

### Question:

> Where do you usually study?

### Answer:

> I usually study at home.

Expand:

> I usually study at home because it's quiet and comfortable. I normally study in the evening when I have fewer distractions.

---

# 🔥 Part 20 — “Normally / Usually / Generally”

Frequency describe করতে:

> normally

> usually

> generally

> often

> sometimes

> occasionally

> rarely

> hardly ever

---

### Example:

> I usually study in the evening.

> I occasionally go out with my friends.

> I rarely watch television.

---

# 🧠 Part 21 — When Did You Start?

Past experience নিয়ে:

> When did you start learning English?

### Answer:

> I started learning English seriously a few years ago.

Expand:

> I started learning English seriously a few years ago because I realized it was important for my studies and career.

---

# 🔥 Part 22 — How Long Have You...?

Duration:

> How long have you been learning English?

### Answer:

> I've been learning English for several years.

More:

> I've been learning English for several years, but recently I've been focusing more on speaking.

---

# 🧠 Part 23 — Why Questions

“Why?” প্রশ্নের answer formula:

# **REASON → EXPLANATION → EXAMPLE**

### Question:

> Why do you like programming?

### Answer:

> I like programming because I enjoy solving problems. It gives me a chance to build useful things from scratch. For example, I can create a website or an application by writing code.

---

# 🔥 Part 24 — “Because” বারবার ব্যবহার না করে

Instead of:

> I like it because...

> I like it because...

> I like it because...

Use:

> One reason is...

> Another reason is...

> The main reason is...

> What I like most is...

> That's mainly because...

---

### Example:

> There are several reasons why I like programming. One reason is that I enjoy solving problems. Another reason is that I can create useful applications.

---

# 🧠 Part 25 — Giving Examples

Answer strong করতে example দাও:

> For example,...

> For instance,...

> A good example is...

> For example, last week...

---

### Example:

> I like learning practical skills. For example, I've recently been working on several programming projects.

---

# 🔥 Part 26 — Personal Experience

Opinion + experience:

> In my experience,...

> From my experience,...

> I've found that...

### Examples:

> In my experience, practicing every day is more effective than studying once a week.

> I've found that speaking regularly helps me improve faster.

---

# 🧠 Part 27 — “I've Found That...”

Excellent intermediate structure:

> I've found that consistency is more important than studying for long hours.

> I've found that practicing with real examples helps me remember things better.

---

# 🔥 Part 28 — Agreeing Naturally

শুধু:

> Yes.

এর বদলে:

> Exactly.

> Absolutely.

> I completely agree.

> That's true.

> I think you're right.

> I feel the same way.

---

### Example:

**A:**

> English is important for career development.

**B:**

> Absolutely. I think it creates a lot of opportunities.

---

# 🧠 Part 29 — Disagreeing Politely

❌

> You're wrong.

Better:

> I see your point, but...

> I understand what you mean, but...

> I'm not sure I completely agree.

> I look at it a little differently.

---

### Example:

> I see your point, but I think working from home isn't suitable for everyone.

---

# 🔥 Part 30 — Clarifying Your Own Answer

কথার মধ্যে নিজের idea clarify:

> What I mean is...

> In other words,...

> Basically,...

> To put it simply,...

---

### Example:

> I prefer working independently. What I mean is that I like having control over my schedule.

---

# 🧠 Part 31 — Correcting Yourself

Speaking-এ mistake হলে থেমে যাবে না।

### Example:

> I went there last Sunday—sorry, I mean last Saturday.

অথবা:

> He works in Dhaka... actually, he used to work there.

Natural speakers-ও self-correction করে।

---

# 🔥 Part 32 — When You Don't Know the Answer

❌ চুপ হয়ে যাবে না।

Use:

> I'm not really sure.

> I'm not certain, but I think...

> I don't know much about that, but...

> I've never really thought about it.

### Example:

> I'm not really sure, but I'd say technology will become even more important in the future.

---

# 🧠 Part 33 — When You Don't Understand the Question

Use:

> Sorry, could you rephrase the question?

**rephrase = অন্যভাবে বলা**

> Could you explain what you mean?

> Sorry, I didn't quite understand the question.

---

# 🔥 Part 34 — Conversation Continuation Formula

একজন কিছু বললে:

# **REACT → COMMENT → QUESTION**

### Example:

**A:**

> I went to Cox's Bazar last weekend.

**B:**

**React:**

> Oh, really?

**Comment:**

> That sounds great.

**Question:**

> How was the trip?

---

Another:

> That's interesting. I've never been there. What did you enjoy most?

---

# 🧠 Part 35 — “That Sounds...”

Reaction দিতে:

> That sounds great.

> That sounds interesting.

> That sounds difficult.

> That sounds exciting.

> That sounds like a lot of fun.

---

# 🔥 Part 36 — Natural Mini Conversation

**A:**

> What do you usually do on weekends?

**B:**

> Well, I usually spend some time with my family and work on personal projects.

**A:**

> What kind of projects?

**B:**

> Mostly programming projects. I'm interested in web development.

**A:**

> That's interesting. How did you get into programming?

**B:**

> I started learning it during university. At first, it was difficult, but I gradually became more interested in it.

**A:**

> What do you enjoy most about it?

**B:**

> I'd say I enjoy solving problems and building things from scratch.

এটাই **natural conversation flow**।

---

# 🎯 Part 37 — Conversation Chain

একটি answer থেকে পরের question তৈরি করো।

### Topic: Music

**Q1:**

> Do you like music?

**A:**

> Yes, I listen to music almost every day.

↓

**Q2:**

> What kind of music do you like?

↓

**Q3:**

> When do you usually listen to it?

↓

**Q4:**

> Who is your favorite singer?

↓

**Q5:**

> Why do you like that singer?

↓

**Q6:**

> Have you ever attended a live concert?

এভাবে একটি topic থেকে **5–10 মিনিট conversation** চালানো যায়।

---

# 🧠 Part 38 — Topic: Your Daily Life

Practice:

> What time do you usually wake up?

> What do you do in the morning?

> Where do you usually study/work?

> What do you do in the evening?

> How do you spend your weekends?

> What do you usually do before going to bed?

---

# 🔥 Part 39 — Topic: Technology

> How often do you use your phone?

> What do you mainly use it for?

> Which app do you use most?

> Do you prefer phones or computers?

> How has technology changed your life?

> Do you think people depend too much on technology?

---

# 🧠 Part 40 — Topic: Future

Future questions naturally answer করতে:

> What are your plans for the future?

### Answer:

> Well, I have several goals for the future. First, I'd like to improve my professional skills. I'd also like to become more confident in English. In the long term, I'd like to build a successful career in technology.

---

# 🔥 Part 41 — Short → Medium → Long

## Question:

**What do you do in your free time?**

### Short:

> I read books.

### Medium:

> I usually read books and watch educational videos.

### Long:

> I usually spend my free time reading books, watching educational videos, and working on personal projects. I especially enjoy activities that help me learn something useful. For example, I often work on programming projects when I have a few free hours.

---

# 🧠 Part 42 — The “3 Sentence Rule”

যদি answer ছোট হয়ে যায়, minimum:

### Sentence 1:

**Direct answer**

### Sentence 2:

**Reason**

### Sentence 3:

**Example/detail**

### Example:

> I enjoy traveling.
> I like it because I can experience new places and cultures.
> For example, whenever I visit a new place, I try the local food and talk to local people.

এটা speaking practice-এর জন্য excellent।

---

# 🔥 Part 43 — The “5 Sentence Rule”

আরও fluent হতে:

1. Direct answer
2. Reason
3. Detail
4. Example
5. Personal opinion/conclusion

### Example:

> I prefer studying in the morning.
> I feel more focused at that time.
> There are usually fewer distractions.
> For example, I can study difficult topics more easily before starting my daily activities.
> So overall, morning is probably the best time for me to learn.

---

# 🏆 Part 44 — 10 Golden Conversation Expressions

এগুলো speaking-এ repeatedly ব্যবহার করো:

1. **Well, I'd say...**
2. **To be honest,...**
3. **Actually,...**
4. **It depends on...**
5. **One reason is...**
6. **For example,...**
7. **In my experience,...**
8. **What I mean is...**
9. **What about you?**
10. **That's an interesting question.**

---

# 🎤 Part 45 — Speaking Practice

## Topic 1: Your Hobby

Answer:

> What do you do in your free time?

Then expand:

> Why do you enjoy it?

> How often do you do it?

> When did you start?

> Who do you usually do it with?

---

## Topic 2: Your City

> Where do you live?

> What do you like about your city?

> What's one problem?

> How could it be improved?

> Would you like to live there in the future?

---

## Topic 3: Technology

> What device do you use most?

> Why do you use it?

> How often do you use it?

> What are its advantages?

> What are its disadvantages?

---

# 🧩 Part 46 — Real Conversation Challenge

এবার একটা answer থেকে **নিজেই follow-up question তৈরি করবে।**

### Example:

> I usually spend my weekends at home.

নিজেকে জিজ্ঞেস করো:

> Why do you stay home?

Answer:

> Because I can relax and work on personal projects.

Next:

> What kind of projects?

Answer:

> Mostly programming projects.

Next:

> Why do you enjoy programming?

Answer:

> Because I enjoy solving problems and building useful things.

এইভাবে **একটি answer → নতুন question → নতুন answer**।

এটাই natural speaking-এর core skill।

---

# 🏆 Lesson 55 Golden Formula

সবচেয়ে গুরুত্বপূর্ণ:

# **ANSWER → REASON → DETAIL → EXAMPLE → FOLLOW-UP QUESTION**

### Example:

> **Yes, I enjoy traveling.**
> **I like it because I can explore new places.**
> **I also enjoy trying different foods and meeting new people.**
> **For example, when I visit a new city, I usually explore the local markets.**
> **What about you? Do you enjoy traveling?**

🔥 এই pattern practice করলে তোমার answer আর **one-line answer** থাকবে না।

# 🟢 English Speaking Course — Lesson 56

# 💭 Opinions, Reasons & Personal Views

**Level:** Intermediate → Upper-Intermediate  
**Main Focus:** নিজের **মতামত clearly বলা**, কেন এমন মনে হয় তা explain করা, example দেওয়া, অন্যের opinion-এর সাথে agree/disagree করা এবং **balanced opinion** দেওয়া।

**Lesson 54 — Real-Life Situations**  
→ Problem explain + request + solution

→ Answer expand + follow-up question

**Lesson 56 — Opinions, Reasons & Personal Views**  
→ এখন **“What do you think?” / “Do you agree?” / “What is your opinion?”** ধরনের প্রশ্নের natural এবং detailed answer শিখব।

---

# 🎯 Lesson 56-এর Main Goal

আজকের lesson শেষে তুমি পারবে:

- নিজের opinion দিতে
    
- strong opinion ও soft opinion দিতে
    
- reason explain করতে
    
- example দিতে
    
- agree করতে
    
- politely disagree করতে
    
- দুই দিকের argument বলতে
    
- balanced opinion দিতে
    
- নিজের opinion পরিবর্তন/clarify করতে
    
- 2–3 মিনিট ধরে একটি topic নিয়ে কথা বলতে
    

---

# 🧠 Part 1 — Opinion কী?

**Opinion = মতামত**

যেমন:

> I think learning English is important.

> In my opinion, social media has both advantages and disadvantages.

> Personally, I prefer studying in the morning.

---

# 🔥 Part 2 — Basic Opinion Expressions

সবচেয়ে useful:

> I think...

> I believe...

> In my opinion,...

> Personally, I think...

> I feel that...

> From my point of view,...

> As far as I'm concerned,...

> I'd say...

---

### Examples:

> I think technology makes our lives easier.

> In my opinion, exercise is essential for a healthy lifestyle.

> Personally, I prefer working in a team.

> From my point of view, education should be more practical.

---

# 🧠 Part 3 — Opinion Strength

সব opinion একই strength-এর নয়।

### Weak / Soft:

> I think...

> I suppose...

> I would say...

> I feel...

### Medium:

> I believe...

> In my opinion...

> From my point of view...

### Strong:

> I strongly believe...

> I'm convinced that...

> There's no doubt that...

---

### Example:

**Soft:**

> I think exercise is important.

**Strong:**

> I strongly believe that regular exercise is essential for a healthy life.

---

# 🔥 Part 4 — “I'd Say”

খুব natural expression:

> I'd say learning English is extremely useful.

> I'd say technology has changed our lives significantly.

> I'd say working remotely can be beneficial.

এটি speaking-এ খুব natural শোনায়।

---

# 🧠 Part 5 — Opinion Formula

একটি strong opinion-এর basic formula:

# **OPINION → REASON → EXAMPLE → CONCLUSION**

### Example:

> **I think learning English is very important.**  
> **The main reason is that English is widely used in education and professional communication.**  
> **For example, many useful courses and technical resources are available in English.**  
> **So, I believe improving English can create more opportunities.**

---

# 🔥 Part 6 — Give Reasons

শুধু:

> I think it's good.

বলবে না।

তারপর:

> The main reason is...

> One reason is...

> Another reason is...

> That's mainly because...

> This is because...

---

### Example:

> I prefer online learning. One reason is that it's flexible. Another reason is that I can learn at my own pace.

---

# 🧠 Part 7 — “One Reason Is...”

একটা reason:

> One reason is that it's convenient.

দুইটা:

> One reason is that it's convenient. Another reason is that it's affordable.

তিনটা:

> One reason is convenience. Another reason is cost. A third reason is flexibility.

---

# 🔥 Part 8 — Adding More Ideas

একটা idea-এর পরে:

> Also,...

> Besides,...

> In addition,...

> Furthermore,...

> What's more,...

> Another point is that...

---

### Example:

> Online learning is flexible. In addition, it's often more affordable than traditional classes.

---

# 🧠 Part 9 — Giving Examples

Opinion strong করতে example দাও:

> For example,...

> For instance,...

> A good example is...

> Take ... for example.

---

### Example:

> Technology has made communication easier. For example, we can communicate with someone on the other side of the world within seconds.

---

# 🔥 Part 10 — Personal Experience as Evidence

তোমার নিজের experience:

> In my experience,...

> From my experience,...

> I've found that...

### Example:

> In my experience, practicing English every day is more effective than studying grammar for several hours once a week.

---

# 🧠 Part 11 — Agreeing

কেউ opinion দিলে:

### Simple:

> I agree.

### Natural:

> I completely agree.

> I absolutely agree.

> Exactly.

> That's true.

> I think you're right.

> I feel the same way.

---

### Example:

**A:**

> Exercise is important for everyone.

**B:**

> Absolutely. I think regular exercise can improve both physical and mental well-being.

---

# 🔥 Part 12 — Strong Agreement

> I couldn't agree more.

Meaning:

**আমি পুরোপুরি একমত।**

### Example:

> A good education is essential for a country's development.

> I couldn't agree more.

---

# 🧠 Part 13 — Partial Agreement

সবকিছুর সাথে agree না করলে:

> I agree to some extent.

> I agree up to a point.

> I partly agree.

> I can see why you think that.

---

### Example:

> I agree to some extent, but I think there are some exceptions.

---

# 🔥 Part 14 — Disagreeing Politely

❌ Don't say:

> You're wrong.

Better:

> I'm not sure I agree.

> I don't completely agree.

> I see your point, but...

> I understand what you mean, but...

> I look at it a little differently.

---

### Example:

> I see your point, but I think technology can actually reduce stress when it's used properly.

---

# 🧠 Part 15 — “I See Your Point”

Very useful polite disagreement:

> I see your point, but I have a slightly different opinion.

Meaning:

**তোমার point বুঝতে পারছি, কিন্তু আমার মতামত কিছুটা ভিন্ন।**

---

# 🔥 Part 16 — Giving Two Sides

Advanced opinion:

# **On the one hand → On the other hand**

### Example:

> On the one hand, social media helps people stay connected. On the other hand, excessive use can waste a lot of time.

এই structure **balanced answer**-এর জন্য খুব important।

---

# 🧠 Part 17 — “On the One Hand”

একটি side:

> On the one hand, online learning is flexible.

তারপর:

> On the other hand, students may miss face-to-face interaction.

---

# 🔥 Part 18 — Advantages & Disadvantages

Opinion question হলে:

> One major advantage is...

> Another benefit is...

> However, one disadvantage is...

> The main drawback is...

### Example:

> One major advantage of remote work is flexibility. However, one drawback is that some people may feel isolated.

---

# 🧠 Part 19 — Balanced Opinion

সবচেয়ে useful structure:

# **POSITIVE → NEGATIVE → YOUR FINAL OPINION**

### Example:

> I think social media has both advantages and disadvantages. On the one hand, it allows people to communicate easily and access information quickly. On the other hand, excessive use can waste time and affect concentration. Overall, I think social media is useful if we use it responsibly.

---

# 🔥 Part 20 — “It Depends”

সব topic-এর answer black and white নয়।

> It depends on the situation.

> It depends on how it's used.

> It depends on the person.

### Example:

> Is social media good or bad?

> I'd say it depends on how it's used. Used responsibly, it can be very useful, but excessive use can cause problems.

---

# 🧠 Part 21 — “To Some Extent”

**to some extent = কিছুটা**

### Example:

> I agree with that to some extent.

> Technology has made people less social to some extent.

---

# 🔥 Part 22 — “Generally Speaking”

General opinion:

> Generally speaking, technology has improved our lives.

> Generally speaking, people prefer convenience.

---

# 🧠 Part 23 — “In Most Cases”

> In most cases, online learning is convenient.

> In most cases, people prefer affordable products.

---

# 🔥 Part 24 — Changing Your Opinion

কখনো discussion-এর পরে opinion change হতে পারে।

### Useful:

> I hadn't thought about it that way.

> That's a good point.

> You may be right.

> I might have to reconsider that.

> That actually makes sense.

---

### Example:

**A:**

> Working from home can save commuting time.

**B:**

> That's a good point. I hadn't thought about it that way.

🔥 Very natural English।

---

# 🧠 Part 25 — Clarifying Your Opinion

যদি অন্য ব্যক্তি ভুল বুঝে:

> What I mean is...

> Let me explain what I mean.

> What I'm trying to say is...

### Example:

> I don't mean that technology is bad. What I'm trying to say is that we should use it responsibly.

---

# 🔥 Part 26 — Softening Strong Opinions

Direct:

> Social media is bad.

Better:

> I think excessive use of social media can be harmful.

Even better:

> Personally, I feel that excessive use of social media can have negative effects.

এভাবে opinion **polite + mature** হয়।

---

# 🧠 Part 27 — “I Don't Think...”

Strong statement-এর softer form:

❌

> That's impossible.

Better:

> I don't think that's possible.

❌

> This is a bad idea.

Better:

> I don't think that's a very good idea.

---

# 🔥 Part 28 — Opinion About Technology

### Question:

> Do you think technology has improved our lives?

### Answer:

> Yes, definitely. I believe technology has made our lives more convenient and connected. For example, we can communicate instantly, access information easily, and complete many tasks online. However, I also think excessive dependence on technology can create problems. Overall, I'd say technology has had a positive impact, as long as we use it responsibly.

---

# 🧠 Part 29 — Opinion About Social Media

### Question:

> Is social media good or bad?

### Answer:

> I'd say social media has both positive and negative sides. On the one hand, it helps people communicate, share information, and promote businesses. On the other hand, excessive use can waste time and negatively affect concentration. In my opinion, social media itself isn't necessarily bad; the important thing is how we use it.

---

# 🔥 Part 30 — Opinion About Online Education

### Question:

> Is online education better than traditional education?

### Answer:

> I think both have advantages. Online education is more flexible because students can learn from almost anywhere and often study at their own pace. Traditional education, however, provides more direct interaction with teachers and classmates. So, I wouldn't say one is always better than the other. It depends on the student, the subject, and the learning environment.

Notice:

# **It depends on...**

এখানে very useful।

---

# 🧠 Part 31 — Opinion About Working From Home

### Question:

> Do you prefer working from home?

### Answer:

> Personally, I think working from home can be very convenient. It saves commuting time and gives people more flexibility. However, it can sometimes be difficult to maintain a clear separation between work and personal life. For me, I'd prefer a flexible system where I can work from home when necessary but also meet my colleagues in person.

---

# 🔥 Part 32 — Opinion About Money

### Question:

> Is money the most important thing in life?

### Answer:

> I don't think money is the most important thing in life, although it is certainly important. Money provides security and allows people to meet their basic needs. However, I believe health, relationships, personal satisfaction, and peace of mind are equally important. So, in my opinion, money is a tool for a good life rather than the ultimate goal.

---

# 🧠 Part 33 — Opinion About Success

### Question:

> What does success mean to you?

### Answer:

> Personally, I don't think success is only about earning a lot of money. I'd say success means achieving meaningful goals while maintaining a balanced and satisfying life. For example, someone can be professionally successful but still feel unhappy. So, I think real success should include both professional achievement and personal well-being.

---

# 🔥 Part 34 — Opinion About Education

### Question:

> Should education be more practical?

### Answer:

> Yes, I strongly believe education should include more practical learning. Theoretical knowledge is important, but students also need opportunities to apply what they learn. For example, project-based learning can help students develop problem-solving and communication skills. In my opinion, a balance between theory and practical experience would be ideal.

---

# 🧠 Part 35 — Strong Opinion Structure

যখন opinion question difficult:

# **INTRODUCTION → OPINION → REASON 1 → REASON 2 → EXAMPLE → OTHER SIDE → CONCLUSION**

### Template:

> That's an interesting question. Personally, I believe that ______.
> 
> One of the main reasons is ______.
> 
> Another important point is ______.
> 
> For example, ______.
> 
> However, I understand that ______.
> 
> On the other hand, ______.
> 
> Overall, I'd say ______.

এই template অনেক **IELTS / interview / BCS viva / job interview speaking** situation-এ কাজে লাগবে।

---

# 🔥 Part 36 — 5-Level Opinion Practice

## Topic: Mobile Phones

### Level 1:

> Phones are useful.

### Level 2:

> I think phones are very useful.

### Level 3:

> I think phones are very useful because they make communication easier.

### Level 4:

> I think smartphones are very useful because they make communication and access to information much easier. I use mine every day for both work and study.

### Level 5:

> Personally, I think smartphones have become an essential part of modern life. They make communication, education, and many everyday tasks much easier. For example, we can attend online classes, make payments, and access information within seconds. However, excessive use can become distracting. Overall, I think smartphones are extremely useful as long as we use them responsibly.

---

# 🧠 Part 37 — Common Opinion Questions

আজ এগুলোর answer practice করবে:

### Technology

1. Is technology good for society?
    
2. Do people depend too much on smartphones?
    
3. Has the internet improved education?
    

### Education

4. Should education be free?
    
5. Is practical knowledge more important than theoretical knowledge?
    
6. Should students use AI for learning?
    

### Work

7. Is working from home better?
    
8. Is salary more important than job satisfaction?
    
9. Should people change jobs frequently?
    

### Lifestyle

10. Is exercise necessary?
    
11. Is city life better than village life?
    
12. Is a busy lifestyle healthy?
    

### Social Media

13. Is social media useful?
    
14. Does social media waste time?
    
15. Should children use social media?
    

---

# 🎤 Part 38 — 2-Minute Speaking Practice

## Topic:

# **“Is Technology Good or Bad?”**

এই structure follow করবে:

### 1️⃣ Opinion

> Personally, I think...

### 2️⃣ Reason 1

> One major reason is...

### 3️⃣ Reason 2

> Another reason is...

### 4️⃣ Example

> For example,...

### 5️⃣ Negative side

> However,...

### 6️⃣ Balanced view

> On the other hand,...

### 7️⃣ Conclusion

> Overall, I'd say...

---

# 🧩 Part 39 — Debate Practice

## Topic:

# **“Social Media Does More Harm Than Good.”**

### Agree side:

> I agree with this statement because...

### Disagree side:

> I don't completely agree because...

### Balanced:

> I think both sides have valid points. On the one hand... On the other hand...

---

# 🔥 Part 40 — Useful Debate Expressions

### Starting:

> I'd like to point out that...

> I believe that...

> From my perspective...

### Adding:

> Furthermore,...

> In addition,...

> Another important point is...

### Contrasting:

> However,...

> On the other hand,...

> Having said that,...

### Example:

> For example,...

### Conclusion:

> Overall,...

> In conclusion,...

> Taking everything into account,...

---

# 🧠 Part 41 — “Having Said That”

Meaning:

**এটা বলার পরও / তারপরও**

### Example:

> Online learning is very convenient. Having said that, it may not be suitable for everyone.

Very useful for balanced opinions।

---

# 🔥 Part 42 — “Taking Everything Into Account”

সব দিক বিবেচনা করে:

> Taking everything into account, I think online education is a useful alternative to traditional education.

এটা conclusion-এর জন্য excellent।

---

# 🧠 Part 43 — Don't Give Extreme Opinions Too Quickly

### Too extreme:

> Technology is always good.

> Technology is always bad.

Better:

> I think technology has both benefits and drawbacks.

কারণ real-world topic-এ **balanced answer** বেশি natural এবং mature।

---

# 🏆 Part 44 — Golden Opinion Formula

মনে রাখো:

# **OPINION → REASON → REASON → EXAMPLE → OTHER SIDE → CONCLUSION**

### Example:

> **Personally, I think social media can be very useful.**  
> **One reason is that it makes communication easier.**  
> **Another reason is that it provides quick access to information.**  
> **For example, people can learn new skills through educational content.**  
> **However, excessive use can waste time and affect concentration.**  
> **Overall, I think social media is beneficial when it's used responsibly.**

---

# 🎯 Part 45 — Your Speaking Challenge

আজ এই 5টি topic-এ **প্রতিটিতে 1–2 মিনিট** কথা বলবে:

### Topic 1

**Is technology making life easier?**

### Topic 2

**Is social media useful or harmful?**

### Topic 3

**Is city life better than village life?**

### Topic 4

**Is money important for happiness?**

### Topic 5

**Should students learn practical skills at school/university?**

প্রতিটি answer-এ অবশ্যই ব্যবহার করবে:

- **I think / Personally**
    
- **One reason is...**
    
- **Another reason is...**
    
- **For example...**
    
- **However...**
    
- **Overall...**
    

---

# 🏆 Lesson 56 Golden Rule

যখন কেউ জিজ্ঞেস করবে:

> **“What do you think?”**

শুধু:

> I think it's good.

বলবে না।

বরং:

> **Personally, I think it's beneficial.**  
> **One of the main reasons is...**  
> **Another important point is...**  
> **For example,...**  
> **However, there are some drawbacks.**  
> **Overall, I'd say...**

এটাই **basic opinion → strong speaking answer**।

---

---
# 🟢 English Speaking Course — Lesson 57

# 🗣️ Agreeing, Disagreeing & Discussion

**Level:** Intermediate → Upper-Intermediate
**Main Focus:** অন্যের কথা শুনে **agree, partially agree, disagree, counter-point, question এবং discussion চালিয়ে যাওয়া**।

### 🔗 Previous lesson connection

**Lesson 55 — Asking & Answering Questions Naturally**
→ conversation কীভাবে চালাতে হয়

**Lesson 56 — Opinions, Reasons & Personal Views**
→ নিজের opinion + reason + example দিতে শেখা

**Lesson 57 — Agreeing, Disagreeing & Discussion**
→ এখন **অন্যের opinion-এর সাথে naturalভাবে response** করা শিখবে।

---

# 🎯 Lesson 57-এর Main Goal

আজকের lesson শেষে তুমি পারবে:

* কারও কথায় agree করতে
* strong agreement দিতে
* partially agree করতে
* politely disagree করতে
* নিজের কারণ দিতে
* counter-argument দিতে
* অন্যের point acknowledge করতে
* discussion চালিয়ে যেতে
* conversation-এ follow-up question করতে
* 5–10 মিনিট একটি topic নিয়ে discussion করতে

---

# 🧠 Part 1 — Discussion কী?

**Discussion = কোনো topic নিয়ে দুই বা তার বেশি মানুষের মধ্যে মতামত বিনিময়।**

Example:

**A:**

> I think working from home is better.

**B:**

> I agree. It saves commuting time.

**A:**

> Exactly. It also gives people more flexibility.

এখানে দুজনই শুধু answer দিচ্ছে না—**একজনের কথার ওপর আরেকজন কথা build করছে।**

---

# 🔥 Part 2 — The Basic Discussion Formula

মনে রাখবে:

# **LISTEN → REACT → AGREE/DISAGREE → REASON → EXAMPLE → QUESTION**

### Example:

> I see your point. **(REACT)**
> I agree that online learning is convenient. **(AGREE)**
> The main reason is that students can study from anywhere. **(REASON)**
> For example, they can attend classes from home. **(EXAMPLE)**
> What do you think about the lack of face-to-face interaction? **(QUESTION)**

এটাই natural discussion।

---

# 🧠 Part 3 — Basic Agreement

সবচেয়ে সহজ:

> I agree.

> That's true.

> Exactly.

> Absolutely.

> Definitely.

---

### Example:

**A:**

> Exercise is important.

**B:**

> Absolutely. It helps people stay healthy.

---

# 🔥 Part 4 — Strong Agreement

আরও strong:

> I completely agree.

> I totally agree.

> I strongly agree.

> I couldn't agree more.

> I absolutely agree with you.

---

### Example:

**A:**

> Learning English can create more career opportunities.

**B:**

> I couldn't agree more. English is useful in many professional environments.

---

# 🧠 Part 5 — “Exactly”

কেউ এমন কিছু বলেছে যার সাথে তুমি পুরোপুরি agree:

> Exactly.

> That's exactly what I think.

> Exactly. I was thinking the same thing.

---

### Example:

**A:**

> Students need more practical experience.

**B:**

> Exactly. Practical experience helps students understand how to apply their knowledge.

---

# 🔥 Part 6 — “I Feel the Same Way”

অন্যের opinion-এর সাথে নিজের opinion match করলে:

> I feel the same way.

> I think the same thing.

> I have a similar opinion.

### Example:

**A:**

> I prefer studying in the morning.

**B:**

> I feel the same way. I can concentrate better in the morning.

---

# 🧠 Part 7 — Partial Agreement

সবটা agree না করলে:

> I agree to some extent.

> I partly agree.

> I agree up to a point.

> I agree with you to some extent.

---

### Example:

**A:**

> Online learning is better than traditional learning.

**B:**

> I agree to some extent, but I think traditional classrooms still have some important advantages.

🔥 এই expression খুব important।

---

# 🔥 Part 8 — “I See Your Point”

Disagree করার আগে অন্যের point acknowledge করো:

> I see your point.

> I understand what you mean.

> I can see why you think that.

তারপর:

> However,...

> But I think...

> Personally, I feel...

---

### Example:

> I see your point, but I think working from home can sometimes reduce communication between team members.

এটি:

**polite + mature + natural English।**

---

# 🧠 Part 9 — Polite Disagreement

❌ Avoid:

> You're wrong.

> That's wrong.

> That's nonsense.

> You don't understand.

Better:

> I'm not sure I agree.

> I don't completely agree.

> I'm afraid I disagree.

> I see it differently.

> I have a slightly different opinion.

---

# 🔥 Part 10 — “I'm Not Sure I Agree”

Very natural:

> I'm not sure I agree with that.

### Example:

**A:**

> Social media is mostly harmful.

**B:**

> I'm not sure I agree with that. I think it depends on how people use it.

---

# 🧠 Part 11 — “I Look at It Differently”

Polite disagreement:

> I look at it a little differently.

### Example:

> I understand your point, but I look at it a little differently. I think technology can actually improve people's lives when it's used properly.

---

# 🔥 Part 12 — Give a Counter-Point

Disagree করার পরে reason:

> The thing is,...

> The main issue is...

> The problem is that...

> One thing to consider is...

---

### Example:

> I see your point, but one thing to consider is the cost.

> I partly agree, but the main issue is that not everyone has access to reliable internet.

---

# 🧠 Part 13 — “One Thing to Consider Is...”

Very useful discussion phrase:

> One thing to consider is the cost.

> One thing to consider is the impact on students.

> One thing to consider is the amount of time required.

---

# 🔥 Part 14 — Giving Another Perspective

> Another way of looking at it is...

> We should also consider...

> It's also worth considering...

### Example:

> Another way of looking at it is that online education gives students more flexibility.

---

# 🧠 Part 15 — “It's Worth Considering”

**worth considering = বিবেচনা করা উচিত**

> It's worth considering the long-term effects.

> It's worth considering both sides of the issue.

---

# 🔥 Part 16 — Asking Follow-Up Questions

Discussion চালানোর জন্য question করো:

> What do you think?

> What makes you say that?

> Why do you think so?

> Could you explain what you mean?

> What about...?

> How do you see it?

> Do you think that's always true?

---

# 🧠 Part 17 — “What Makes You Say That?”

এটি খুব powerful discussion question।

### Example:

**A:**

> I think social media is harmful.

**B:**

> What makes you say that?

**A:**

> Because people spend too much time on it.

এখন discussion আরও deep হবে।

---

# 🔥 Part 18 — “Do You Think That's Always True?”

General statement challenge করতে:

**A:**

> Online learning is better.

**B:**

> Do you think that's always true?

এটি polite way-তে counter-question।

---

# 🧠 Part 19 — Asking for Evidence

যদি opinion-এর basis জানতে চাও:

> What makes you think that?

> Do you have any examples?

> What evidence do you have?

> Have you experienced that yourself?

Conversation context অনুযায়ী ব্যবহার করবে।

---

# 🔥 Part 20 — Agree + Add Something

শুধু:

> I agree.

বললে conversation weak হয়।

Better:

> I agree, and I'd also add that...

### Example:

> I agree, and I'd also add that practical experience is important.

---

# 🧠 Part 21 — Agree + Example

> I completely agree. For example, students can learn much faster when they actually work on real projects.

---

# 🔥 Part 22 — Disagree + Reason

Formula:

# **ACKNOWLEDGE → DISAGREE → REASON**

### Example:

> I understand your point, but I don't completely agree. I think working from home can be more productive for people who need a quiet environment.

---

# 🧠 Part 23 — Disagree + Counter-Example

### Example:

**A:**

> People can't be productive at home.

**B:**

> I see your point, but I'm not sure that's always true. For example, many people can concentrate better at home because they have fewer interruptions.

🔥 This is a strong discussion response।

---

# 🔥 Part 24 — “Not Necessarily”

কেউ general statement দিলে:

> Not necessarily.

Meaning:

**অবশ্যই এমন নয়।**

### Example:

**A:**

> Working longer hours means working harder.

**B:**

> Not necessarily. Productivity depends more on how effectively you use your time.

Very useful।

---

# 🧠 Part 25 — “It Depends”

Discussion-এর most useful phrase:

> It depends.

আরও ভালো:

> It depends on the situation.

> It depends on the person.

> It depends on how it's used.

---

### Example:

**A:**

> Is social media good?

**B:**

> It depends on how it's used.

---

# 🔥 Part 26 — “To Some Extent”

Partial agreement:

> I agree with you to some extent.

### Example:

> I agree that technology can be distracting to some extent, but I don't think technology itself is the problem.

---

# 🧠 Part 27 — “However”

Opposite idea introduce করতে:

> I agree that technology is useful. However, it can also create problems if people depend on it too much.

---

# 🔥 Part 28 — “Having Said That”

আগের কথার পর opposite/extra point:

> Online education is very convenient. Having said that, it may not work equally well for every student.

---

# 🧠 Part 29 — “At the Same Time”

দুইটা truth একসাথে:

> Technology saves time. At the same time, it can create distractions.

---

# 🔥 Part 30 — “On the Other Hand”

দুই side:

> On the one hand, social media helps people communicate. On the other hand, it can waste a lot of time.

---

# 🧠 Part 31 — Discussion Connectors

| Purpose       | Expressions                    |
| ------------- | ------------------------------ |
| Agree         | I agree, Exactly, Absolutely   |
| Partial agree | To some extent, I partly agree |
| Disagree      | I don't completely agree       |
| Contrast      | However, On the other hand     |
| Add           | Also, In addition, Furthermore |
| Example       | For example, For instance      |
| Reason        | Because, One reason is         |
| Clarify       | What I mean is                 |
| Question      | What do you think?             |
| Conclusion    | Overall, In my opinion         |

---

# 🔥 Part 32 — Mini Discussion: Online Learning

### A:

> I think online learning is better than traditional learning.

### B:

> I agree that online learning is more flexible. Students can study from anywhere.

### A:

> Exactly. They can also learn at their own pace.

### B:

> That's true. However, I think traditional classrooms have one major advantage: direct interaction.

### A:

> That's a good point. But don't you think video calls can solve that problem?

### B:

> To some extent, yes. But I don't think online communication is always the same as face-to-face interaction.

### A:

> I see what you mean.

🔥 Notice the flow:

**Agree → Add → However → Counter-point → Question → Partial agreement**

---

# 🧠 Part 33 — Mini Discussion: Social Media

### A:

> I think social media does more harm than good.

### B:

> I see your point, but I'm not sure I completely agree.

### A:

> Why not?

### B:

> Because it depends on how people use it. For example, students can use social media to find educational content.

### A:

> That's true, but many people waste hours scrolling.

### B:

> Absolutely. That's a serious problem. However, I think the solution is responsible use rather than avoiding social media completely.

### A:

> That's an interesting way of looking at it.

---

# 🔥 Part 34 — Mini Discussion: Working From Home

### A:

> Working from home is better than working in an office.

### B:

> I agree to some extent.

### A:

> Why only to some extent?

### B:

> Because it depends on the type of job. Some jobs can be done effectively from home, while others require direct teamwork.

### A:

> That's a good point.

### B:

> I also think personality matters. Some people work better independently, while others prefer working with a team.

---

# 🧠 Part 35 — Discussion Mistakes

### ❌ Mistake 1: Too direct

> You're wrong.

### ✅ Better:

> I see your point, but I look at it differently.

---

### ❌ Mistake 2:

> I agree.

Then silence.

### ✅ Better:

> I agree, and I'd also add that...

---

### ❌ Mistake 3:

> No, that's not true.

### ✅ Better:

> I'm not sure that's always the case.

---

### ❌ Mistake 4:

> Why?

Repeatedly.

### ✅ Better:

> What makes you say that?

---

# 🔥 Part 36 — “That's a Good Point”

কেউ ভালো argument দিলে:

> That's a good point.

> That's an interesting point.

> You have a point there.

> I hadn't considered that.

---

### Example:

> I hadn't considered that. You're right that cost is an important factor.

---

# 🧠 Part 37 — “You Have a Point There”

Meaning:

**তোমার কথায় যুক্তি আছে।**

### Example:

> You have a point there. Maybe we should consider the long-term effects.

---

# 🔥 Part 38 — When You Change Your Mind

Discussion-এ নিজের opinion update করা completely okay।

Use:

> You've convinced me.

> You may be right.

> I hadn't thought about it that way.

> That's changed my perspective.

> I think you have a point.

---

### Example:

> I hadn't thought about it that way. You may be right.

---

# 🧠 Part 39 — Strong Discussion Structure

যখন তোমাকে 2–3 মিনিট কথা বলতে হবে:

# **POSITION → REASON → EXAMPLE → COUNTER-POINT → RESPONSE → CONCLUSION**

### Example:

> Personally, I think remote work is beneficial.
>
> One reason is that it saves commuting time.
>
> For example, employees can use that extra time for work or personal activities.
>
> However, some people argue that remote work reduces teamwork.
>
> I understand that concern, but I think modern communication tools can solve some of these problems.
>
> Overall, I think remote work is a good option when the job and employee are suitable for it.

---

# 🎯 Part 40 — Discussion Topic 1

## **“Is Social Media Good for Society?”**

### Question chain:

> Do you think social media is useful?

↓

> Why?

↓

> What are its advantages?

↓

> What are its disadvantages?

↓

> Do people spend too much time on it?

↓

> How can people use it responsibly?

---

# 🎯 Part 41 — Discussion Topic 2

## **“Online Learning vs Traditional Learning”**

Questions:

> Which do you prefer?

> Why?

> What are the advantages of online learning?

> What are the disadvantages?

> Is face-to-face interaction important?

> Can online learning completely replace classrooms?

---

# 🎯 Part 42 — Discussion Topic 3

## **“Money vs Happiness”**

Questions:

> Is money necessary for happiness?

> Can money buy happiness?

> What else is important?

> Is a high-paying job always better?

> What would you choose: high salary or job satisfaction?

---

# 🎯 Part 43 — Discussion Topic 4

## **“Technology and Human Life”**

Questions:

> Has technology improved our lives?

> Are people too dependent on technology?

> Does technology make people lazy?

> What are the biggest benefits?

> What are the biggest risks?

---

# 🎤 Part 44 — 5-Minute Speaking Practice

আজ একটি partner না থাকলেও **নিজেই দুই role** করবে।

### Topic:

# **“Should students use AI for education?”**

### Person A:

> I think students should use AI because it can help them learn faster.

### Person B:

> I agree to some extent, but I think students shouldn't depend on it too much.

### A:

> Why do you think that?

### B:

> Because they still need to develop their own problem-solving skills.

### A:

> That's a good point. But AI can explain difficult topics.

### B:

> Absolutely. I think AI is useful as a learning assistant, but students should still do the thinking themselves.

### A:

> I agree with that.

এই conversation pattern বারবার practice করবে।

---

# 🧠 Part 45 — The 7 Most Important Phrases

যদি আজ সব কিছু মনে রাখতে না পারো, শুধু এই **7টা** শিখো:

### 1.

> **I completely agree.**

### 2.

> **I agree to some extent.**

### 3.

> **I see your point, but...**

### 4.

> **I'm not sure I agree.**

### 5.

> **That's a good point.**

### 6.

> **What makes you say that?**

### 7.

> **It depends on the situation.**

এই 7টা phrase দিয়ে অনেক discussion চালানো যায়।

---

# 🏆 Lesson 57 Golden Formula

একজন কিছু বললে:

# **REACT → AGREE/DISAGREE → REASON → EXAMPLE → COUNTER-POINT → QUESTION**

### Example:

> **I see your point.**
> **I agree to some extent.**
> **The main reason is that...**
> **For example,...**
> **However, we should also consider...**
> **What do you think about that?**

🔥 এই structure আয়ত্ত করলে তুমি শুধু English **বলবে না**, English-এ **discussion করতে পারবে**।

---

↓

→ নিজের opinion explain করা

↓

→ **অন্যের opinion-এর response + counter-point + discussion**

↓

→ **কোনো ঘটনা/অভিজ্ঞতা শুরু থেকে শেষ পর্যন্ত interesting এবং organizedভাবে English-এ বলা** — *Beginning → Background → Main Event → Problem → Action → Result → Feeling*।


---
# 🟢 English Speaking Course — Lesson 58

# 📖 Storytelling & Narration

**Level:** Intermediate → Upper-Intermediate
**Main Focus:** কোনো **ঘটনা, অভিজ্ঞতা, trip, problem, memorable day বা personal story** English-এ শুরু থেকে শেষ পর্যন্ত naturalভাবে বলা।

### 🔗 Previous lesson connection

**Lesson 55 — Asking & Answering Questions**
→ conversation চালানো

**Lesson 56 — Opinions & Personal Views**
→ নিজের opinion explain করা

**Lesson 57 — Agreeing, Disagreeing & Discussion**
→ discussion করা

**Lesson 58 — Storytelling & Narration**
→ এখন একটি **ঘটনা পুরো story হিসেবে organizedভাবে বলা**।

---

# 🎯 Lesson 58-এর Main Goal

আজকের lesson শেষে তুমি পারবে:

* কোনো ঘটনা শুরু থেকে শেষ পর্যন্ত বলতে
* Past tense ব্যবহার করে story বলতে
* ঘটনাগুলো sequence অনুযায়ী সাজাতে
* background explain করতে
* problem describe করতে
* নিজের action বলতে
* result বলতে
* feeling/opinion যোগ করতে
* story interesting করতে
* “then, after that, suddenly, finally” naturalভাবে ব্যবহার করতে

---

# 🧠 Part 1 — Storytelling কী?

Storytelling মানে শুধু:

> I went there.
> I saw something.
> I came home.

না।

Storytelling হলো:

> **What happened + When + Where + What you did + What happened next + How you felt**

---

# 🔥 Part 2 — The 7-Step Story Formula

সবচেয়ে important structure:

# **1. Background**

# **2. Beginning**

# **3. Main Event**

# **4. Problem**

# **5. Action**

# **6. Result**

# **7. Feeling/Lesson**

এই structure মনে রাখবে।

---

# 🧩 Part 3 — Step 1: Background

Story শুরু করার সময়:

> Last week,...

> A few days ago,...

> Last year,...

> When I was in university,...

> One morning,...

> A few months ago,...

---

### Example:

> A few months ago, I had an interesting experience while traveling to another city.

এখন listener বুঝতে পারল **কখন এবং কী নিয়ে story**।

---

# 🔥 Part 4 — Step 2: Setting

**Where + When**

> It happened last Friday at a shopping mall.

> One evening, I was walking home from university.

> Last summer, I went to Cox's Bazar with some friends.

---

### Formula:

# **It happened + time + place.**

> It happened last year at my university.

---

# 🧠 Part 5 — Step 3: Beginning

Story শুরু:

> At first,...

> In the beginning,...

> Initially,...

> Everything was normal until...

### Example:

> At first, everything was going normally.

> Everything was fine until something unexpected happened.

---

# 🔥 Part 6 — “Everything Was Fine Until...”

খুব useful storytelling structure:

> Everything was fine until my phone suddenly stopped working.

> Everything was going well until it started raining.

> Everything was normal until I realized I had lost my wallet.

এতে story interesting হয়।

---

# 🧠 Part 7 — Step 4: Sequence Words

ঘটনা একটার পর একটা বলার জন্য:

> First,...

> Then,...

> Next,...

> After that,...

> A few minutes later,...

> Later,...

> Eventually,...

> Finally,...

---

### Example:

> First, I went to the station. Then, I bought a ticket. After that, I waited for the bus. Finally, the bus arrived.

---

# 🔥 Part 8 — “Suddenly”

Unexpected ঘটনা:

> Suddenly,...

### Examples:

> Suddenly, the lights went out.

> Suddenly, it started raining.

> Suddenly, I heard a strange noise.

---

# 🧠 Part 9 — “All of a Sudden”

আরও natural storytelling:

> All of a sudden, the car stopped.

> All of a sudden, everyone started running.

**All of a sudden = হঠাৎ করে**

---

# 🔥 Part 10 — Step 5: The Problem

Story-তে problem থাকলে:

> Unfortunately,...

> The problem was that...

> The problem started when...

> Things became difficult when...

> That's when...

---

### Example:

> Unfortunately, I realized that I had forgotten my wallet.

আরও natural:

> That's when I realized that I had left my wallet at home.

---

# 🧠 Part 11 — “That's When...”

Very useful:

> That's when I realized I had lost my phone.

> That's when things became difficult.

> That's when I decided to call my friend.

এটি story-কে natural করে।

---

# 🔥 Part 12 — Step 6: What Did You Do?

Action explain:

> So, I decided to...

> I immediately...

> I tried to...

> I called...

> I asked...

> I went back...

---

### Example:

> I immediately called my friend and asked for help.

> I tried restarting my phone, but it didn't work.

---

# 🧠 Part 13 — “Immediately”

কোনো কাজ দ্রুত করলে:

> I immediately called him.

> I immediately went back to the station.

> I immediately asked for help.

---

# 🔥 Part 14 — Step 7: Result

Story শেষের দিকে:

> Eventually,...

> In the end,...

> Finally,...

> Fortunately,...

> Luckily,...

> Unfortunately,...

---

### Examples:

> Eventually, we found the missing bag.

> In the end, everything worked out.

> Fortunately, nobody was hurt.

> Luckily, I found my wallet.

---

# 🧠 Part 15 — “It Worked Out”

Very useful phrase:

> Everything worked out in the end.

Meaning:

**শেষ পর্যন্ত সব ঠিক হয়ে গেল।**

### Example:

> We had several problems during the trip, but everything worked out in the end.

---

# 🔥 Part 16 — Feelings

Story শেষ হলে feeling বলবে:

> I felt relieved.

> I was really happy.

> I was surprised.

> I was nervous.

> I was embarrassed.

> I was disappointed.

> I was excited.

> I was grateful.

---

# 🧠 Part 17 — Useful Feeling Vocabulary

| English      | বাংলা            |
| ------------ | ---------------- |
| excited      | উত্তেজিত/আনন্দিত |
| nervous      | নার্ভাস          |
| worried      | চিন্তিত          |
| surprised    | অবাক             |
| shocked      | হতবাক            |
| embarrassed  | বিব্রত           |
| disappointed | হতাশ             |
| relieved     | স্বস্তি পাওয়া   |
| grateful     | কৃতজ্ঞ           |
| confused     | বিভ্রান্ত        |
| proud        | গর্বিত           |
| frustrated   | বিরক্ত/হতাশ      |

---

# 🔥 Part 18 — “I Was About to...”

কিছু করতে যাচ্ছিলে:

# **I was about to + verb**

### Examples:

> I was about to leave when my friend called me.

> I was about to go home when it started raining.

> I was about to start studying when the electricity went out.

খুব useful storytelling structure।

---

# 🧠 Part 19 — “Just as...”

একটা কাজ করার ঠিক সময়ে অন্য কিছু ঘটলে:

> Just as I was leaving, my friend arrived.

> Just as we were starting dinner, the lights went out.

Meaning:

**ঠিক যখন...**

---

# 🔥 Part 20 — “While...”

একই সময়ে দুই ঘটনা:

> While I was walking home, I saw an accident.

> While we were waiting, it started raining.

### Structure:

**While + past continuous**

> While I was studying,...

---

# 🧠 Part 21 — “When...”

একটি ঘটনা চলছিল, অন্য ঘটনা ঘটল:

> I was sleeping when the phone rang.

> I was walking home when it started raining.

---

# 🔥 Part 22 — Past Simple vs Past Continuous

Storytelling-এর জন্য খুব important।

### Background / ongoing action:

**Past Continuous**

> I was walking home.

### Main event:

**Past Simple**

> It started raining.

---

### Combined:

> I was walking home when it suddenly started raining.

---

# 🧠 Part 23 — Past Perfect in Stories

আগে ঘটে যাওয়া ঘটনা:

> I realized that I had left my phone at home.

এখানে:

**realized** → পরে
**had left** → আগে

---

### Another:

> When I arrived at the station, the bus had already left.

---

# 🔥 Part 24 — “By the Time”

একটা ঘটনা ঘটার আগেই অন্য ঘটনা শেষ:

> By the time I arrived, the meeting had already started.

> By the time we got there, the shop had closed.

---

# 🧠 Part 25 — Making Stories More Natural

শুধু:

> First... Then... Then... Then...

বারবার ব্যবহার করবে না।

এর পরিবর্তে:

> At first,...

> A few minutes later,...

> Meanwhile,...

> Suddenly,...

> Just then,...

> After a while,...

> Eventually,...

---

# 🔥 Part 26 — “Meanwhile”

একই সময়ে অন্য কিছু ঘটছে:

> Meanwhile, my friend was waiting outside.

> Meanwhile, I was trying to solve the problem.

---

# 🧠 Part 27 — “Just Then”

Storytelling-এ:

> Just then, I heard someone calling my name.

> Just then, the bus arrived.

**Just then = ঠিক তখনই**

---

# 🔥 Part 28 — “After a While”

কিছু সময় পরে:

> After a while, the rain stopped.

> After a while, my friend came back.

---

# 🧠 Part 29 — Storytelling Opening Lines

Story শুরু করার 10টি natural way:

1. > A few days ago, something interesting happened.
2. > Last year, I had an unforgettable experience.
3. > I'll never forget the day when...
4. > One of the most memorable experiences I've had was...
5. > A few months ago, I...
6. > It all started when...
7. > One day, while I was...
8. > I remember one time when...
9. > There was one occasion when...
10. > Let me tell you about a time when...

---

# 🔥 Part 30 — “I'll Never Forget...”

Memorable story:

> I'll never forget the day I graduated from university.

> I'll never forget the first time I traveled alone.

> I'll never forget what happened that evening.

---

# 🧠 Part 31 — “It All Started When...”

Interesting opening:

> It all started when I received a phone call from my friend.

> It all started when we decided to take a short trip.

---

# 🔥 Part 32 — Storytelling Example 1

# 📱 Lost Phone

> A few months ago, I had a stressful experience when I lost my phone.
>
> I was visiting a shopping mall with a friend. Everything was normal at first. We walked around for a while and then went to a restaurant.
>
> After we finished eating, I reached into my pocket and realized that my phone wasn't there. That's when I started getting worried.
>
> I immediately checked the restaurant and then went back to the stores we had visited. Meanwhile, my friend tried calling my number.
>
> Fortunately, someone answered the phone. They had found it near one of the shops.
>
> In the end, I got my phone back. I felt extremely relieved and grateful. The experience taught me to be more careful with my belongings.

---

# 🧠 Part 33 — Story Breakdown

উপরের story:

### Background:

> A few months ago...

### Setting:

> I was visiting a shopping mall...

### Problem:

> I realized that my phone wasn't there.

### Action:

> I checked the restaurant...

### Unexpected event:

> Someone answered the phone.

### Result:

> I got my phone back.

### Feeling:

> I felt relieved and grateful.

### Lesson:

> I learned to be more careful.

এটাই storytelling structure।

---

# 🔥 Part 34 — Storytelling Example 2

# 🌧️ Unexpected Rain

> Last week, I was walking home from work when it suddenly started raining.
>
> I hadn't expected rain, so I didn't have an umbrella. At first, I tried to continue walking, but the rain became heavier.
>
> I was about to call a taxi when I saw a small shop nearby. I quickly went inside and waited there.
>
> After about twenty minutes, the rain became lighter. I decided to continue walking home.
>
> Fortunately, I eventually reached home safely. I was tired and a little wet, but I was happy that nothing serious had happened.
>
> Since then, I've started checking the weather before leaving home.

---

# 🧠 Part 35 — Storytelling Example 3

# 🎓 A Memorable Day

> One of the most memorable days of my life was my graduation day.
>
> I had been waiting for that day for a long time. My family and friends were there, so I was extremely excited.
>
> At first, everything went according to plan. We attended the ceremony, took photos, and talked with our teachers.
>
> The most emotional moment was when I received my graduation certificate. At that moment, I realized how much effort I had put into my studies.
>
> After the ceremony, we went out for a meal and celebrated together.
>
> I felt extremely happy and proud that day. It was a special moment because it marked the end of one chapter and the beginning of another.

---

# 🔥 Part 36 — Storytelling Example 4

# 💻 A Computer Problem

> A few weeks ago, I had a serious problem with my computer.
>
> I was working on an important project when the computer suddenly froze. At first, I thought it was a temporary problem, so I waited for a few minutes.
>
> However, nothing happened. I tried restarting the computer, but it became slow again.
>
> I was worried because I had an important deadline. I checked the storage and discovered that it was almost full.
>
> I deleted some unnecessary files and restarted the computer. Fortunately, it started working normally again.
>
> I felt relieved because I didn't lose my project. After that experience, I started keeping regular backups.

---

# 🧠 Part 37 — Adding Details

Story interesting করতে details যোগ করো:

### Instead of:

> I went to a restaurant.

### Better:

> I went to a small restaurant near the university with two of my friends.

---

### Instead of:

> It was raining.

### Better:

> It suddenly started raining heavily, and I didn't have an umbrella.

---

# 🔥 Part 38 — Describe the Scene

Storytelling-এ scene describe করতে:

> It was a busy afternoon.

> The place was crowded.

> Everyone was excited.

> The weather was beautiful.

> It was getting dark.

> The streets were almost empty.

---

# 🧠 Part 39 — Dialogue in Stories

Story interesting করতে ছোট dialogue ব্যবহার করতে পারো।

### Example:

> My friend looked at me and said, “Are you sure this is the right way?”

I laughed and said, “I think so.”

Then we continued walking.

**Tip:** খুব বেশি dialogue দরকার নেই। 1–2টা line যথেষ্ট।

---

# 🔥 Part 40 — “I Remember Thinking...”

নিজের thought বলতে:

> I remember thinking, “What should I do now?”

> I remember thinking that I was going to be late.

> At that moment, I remember thinking that something was wrong.

এটি storytelling-কে আরও natural করে।

---

# 🧠 Part 41 — “At That Moment”

Story-এর গুরুত্বপূর্ণ moment:

> At that moment, I realized that I had made a mistake.

> At that moment, I felt extremely nervous.

> At that moment, I didn't know what to do.

---

# 🔥 Part 42 — “Eventually” vs “Finally”

### Eventually

অনেক কিছু ঘটার **পরে শেষ পর্যন্ত**:

> Eventually, we found the hotel.

### Finally

শেষ step:

> Finally, we reached the hotel.

দুটোই “শেষ পর্যন্ত” অর্থ দিতে পারে, কিন্তু ব্যবহার একটু ভিন্ন।

---

# 🧠 Part 43 — “Luckily” / “Fortunately”

ভালো outcome:

> Luckily, nobody was injured.

> Fortunately, I found my wallet.

---

### Negative:

> Unfortunately, I missed the bus.

> Unfortunately, the shop was closed.

---

# 🔥 Part 44 — Story Conclusion

Story শেষ করার expressions:

> In the end,...

> Eventually,...

> Looking back,...

> Overall,...

> It was a memorable experience because...

> The experience taught me that...

---

# 🧠 Part 45 — “Looking Back”

Past experience নিয়ে reflection:

> Looking back, I think it was a valuable experience.

> Looking back, I realize that I should have been more careful.

Excellent intermediate expression।

---

# 🔥 Part 46 — “I Should Have...”

Past mistake/regret:

# **should have + past participle**

> I should have taken an umbrella.

> I should have checked my phone earlier.

> I should have left home earlier.

---

### Example:

> Looking back, I should have checked the weather before leaving home.

---

# 🧠 Part 47 — “If I Had...”

Past hypothetical:

> If I had checked the weather, I wouldn't have gotten wet.

> If I had left earlier, I wouldn't have missed the bus.

এটি একটু advanced, কিন্তু storytelling-এর জন্য খুব useful।

---

# 🔥 Part 48 — Storytelling Master Template

যেকোনো story-এর জন্য:

> **A few days/weeks/months ago, ...**
>
> **I was...**
>
> **At first, ...**
>
> **Everything was fine until...**
>
> **Suddenly, ...**
>
> **That's when...**
>
> **I immediately...**
>
> **After that, ...**
>
> **Eventually, ...**
>
> **In the end, ...**
>
> **I felt...**
>
> **Looking back, ...**
>
> **The experience taught me that...**

এই template practice করলে অনেক story বলতে পারবে।

---

# 🎯 Part 49 — 10 Story Topics

আজ এগুলো practice করবে:

1. **A memorable day**
2. **A difficult decision**
3. **A mistake you made**
4. **A time you helped someone**
5. **A time someone helped you**
6. **A trip you enjoyed**
7. **A time you got lost**
8. **A time something went wrong**
9. **Your first day at university**
10. **A time you learned something important**

---

# 🎤 Part 50 — 2-Minute Speaking Challenge

## Topic:

# **“A Time When Something Went Wrong”**

এই questions-এর answer দিয়ে story বানাবে:

### 1. When did it happen?

> A few months ago...

### 2. Where were you?

> I was...

### 3. What were you doing?

> I was...

### 4. What went wrong?

> Suddenly...

### 5. What did you do?

> I immediately...

### 6. What happened next?

> After that...

### 7. What was the result?

> Eventually...

### 8. How did you feel?

> I felt...

### 9. What did you learn?

> The experience taught me...

---

# 🧩 Part 51 — Storytelling Practice

## Topic: **“The Day I Missed the Bus”**

শুধু keywords দিয়ে story বানানোর চেষ্টা করো:

> Monday morning
> → late
> → getting ready
> → traffic
> → bus stop
> → bus arrived
> → couldn't catch it
> → called friend
> → took another bus
> → arrived late
> → learned a lesson

এখন এগুলোকে full story-তে convert করবে।

---

# 🏆 Part 52 — Golden Story Example

> Last Monday morning, I had an unexpected problem on my way to university.
>
> I woke up later than usual because I had stayed up late the previous night. I quickly got ready and left home without having breakfast.
>
> When I reached the bus stop, I saw that my bus was already leaving. I tried to catch it, but unfortunately, I missed it.
>
> At first, I was really frustrated because I had an important class that morning. I immediately called a friend and asked about another way to get to university.
>
> After a few minutes, I found another bus and got on it. Unfortunately, there was heavy traffic, so I arrived about thirty minutes late.
>
> In the end, everything was fine, but I was quite stressed that morning. Looking back, I realized that I should have gone to bed earlier and left home on time.
>
> The experience taught me that being prepared and managing my time properly can prevent unnecessary problems.

---

# 🎯 Lesson 58-এর সবচেয়ে গুরুত্বপূর্ণ 15 Expressions

1. **A few days ago...**
2. **It all started when...**
3. **At first...**
4. **Everything was fine until...**
5. **Suddenly...**
6. **All of a sudden...**
7. **That's when...**
8. **I immediately...**
9. **Meanwhile...**
10. **Just then...**
11. **After a while...**
12. **Eventually...**
13. **In the end...**
14. **Looking back...**
15. **The experience taught me that...**

---

# 🏆 Lesson 58 Golden Formula

একটা story বলার সময় মনে রাখবে:

# **BACKGROUND**

↓

# **BEGINNING**

↓

# **MAIN EVENT**

↓

# **PROBLEM**

↓

# **ACTION**

↓

# **RESULT**

↓

# **FEELING**

↓

# **LESSON**

### Short version:

> **When/Where → What happened → Problem → What I did → Result → Feeling → Lesson**

---

→ discussion করা

→ **ঘটনা organized এবং interestingভাবে বলা**

→ **শুধু story বলা নয়; মানুষ, place, emotion, action, atmosphere এবং personal reaction যোগ করে একটি experience আরও বিস্তারিত ও naturalভাবে describe করা।**
